## Question 110 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2020-gs1-q110-unacademy**
**Q19. Customs and traditions suppress reason leading to obscurantism. Do you agree? (250 words, 15 marks) Tag: Salient features of Indian Society.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to start by defining** **customs and tradition and how both are** **interplay of each other.**-**InBody,elaboratethosecaseswheretradition** **and customs have caused exploitation or** **above human values. Elaborate cases where** **tradition and customs have valued human** **rights and how they have been integral in** **involving human values.**-**Conclude with the fact that customs and** **traditions are changing with time with some** **landmark judgments of Courts.** **Answer:** **Customs**and**tradition**are a common way of doing things. It is something that many people do and have done for a long time. Usually, the people come from the same country, culture, or religion. Customs and traditions are common ways of doing things continued over a long period of time and are cited as having a religious basis. They are indeed a standard way of performing rituals but may lead to Obscurantism if not examined rationally.**Cases where blind following of tradition has caused**

## **exploitation**

- **Character Test:** The Kanjarbhats are a denotified tribe that are governed by their own set of codified rules and a caste panchayat. As part of the Kanjarbhat caste tradition, a bride is subjected to a “character test” on the wedding night.
- **Lack of access to safe abortion clinics,** particularly public hospitals, and humiliation and attitudes towards women, especially young, unmarried women seeking abortion.
- **“Tradition”** is indeed often used to justify discrimination and crackdowns on rights and is easily hijacked by nations determined to flout the rights of groups and to quash broader social, political,andlegalfreedoms.Insuchenvironments, “tradition” subordinates human rights.
- **“Values”** are sometimes used to justify forced marriages in Afghanistan, virginity testing in Indonesia, “honor crimes” in Iraq, and marital rape in Kyrgyzstan, thus, violating human rights.
- **For LGBT people,** the language of traditional values tends to cast homosexuality as a moral issue, and not a rights issue.
- **Caste discrimination** is perpetuated even today in some places in the name of customs and purity.
- **Religion against the Right to Equality**like the Sabarimala case or Haji Ali Dargah,**where women** **are strictly not allowed.** **Butsomecustomsandtraditionsarewellthoughtand** **are aimed at maintaining social harmony, wellbeing,**

## **like**

- Customsandmannersaretheexpressionsofethics and social conduct in a society.
- **Indian culture is dominating** the whole world today because of its rich culture. When the whole world is reeling under the Covid-19 pandemic threat, western countries are adopting the Indian traditions which include many forms of greetings, and etiquette like washing the hands and feet before entering the house.
- **Family life, and Values:** Prostrating before parents and elders which is an act of respect. Young people bow down and touch the feet of the elders, parents, and teachers. In turn, the elders give blessings by placing their hands on the head of the young people.
- **\Marriage:** Sanctity of marriage is prevalent in many cultures like in India, Institution of marriage is considered as pure and sanctum.
- **Community Value:**Respect for elders, living in community help maintaining social harmony and intermingling of many cultures. Thus, religious traditions and customs are changing with the change of time. The discriminatory nature of traditions is questioned time to time like in**Sabrimala**casewhereSupremeCourtofIndia’sjudgmentgreased**the wheels of social integration and breathed life into** **feminist jurisprudence.** In fact, constitution of India has guaranteed Fundamental Rights overlooking the orthodox traditions.

## Question 111 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2020-gs1-q111-unacademy**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to show digitalization in** **education, its importance and steps taken to** **foster that.**-**In Body,**-**Mention some digital schemes by GOI** **in the education sector, their impact and** **aim.**-**Elaborate impediments to achieve the** **aim behind digital schemes.**-**Conclude by showing the significance of the** **digital initiatives.** **Answer:**Digitalization is**the trending term in the 21st century.**Right from a child to an experienced old person, they are surrounded by**laptops, computers, tablets** **and smartphones.**Educational system is also getting influenced due to the digitalization era. Education is the pillar of any country. The educational system is going through rapid transformation due to which, the children of this generation are not limited to traditional textbooks. To promote digital initiatives, the Government of India has taken many initiatives.**Some of the initiatives in terms of school education**

## **are as follows**

- **e-Basta:**To be consistent with the Government’s Digital India initiative, this project has been created to make**schoolbooks approachable, easy-** **to-use, and user-friendly.** - The main idea is to bring various publishers and schools together on the same stand.
- **Saransh-**CBSE Board initiated an**e-facility titled** **‘Saransh’ CBSE** affiliated schools.
- Ithelpstheschoolstoassesstheirperformance and they can compare it against all CBSE schools at various levels.
- With the help of this online facility, schools can find areas of enhancement in students, teachers and curriculum and work on necessary measures to make changes.
- **E-Pathshala:** It is an app developed by NCERT.
- It addresses a wide audience and also helps in bridging the digital divide between urban and rural areas.
- It has a variety of accessibility options.
- **e-Education:** Digital India aims to connect all the schools in India through Wi-Fi and broadband facilities.
- This ensures that all the schools, be it in urban or rural, are able to access the state of art facilities and become familiar with the latest technologies.
- **SWAYAM:** The ‘Study Webs of Active Learning for Young Aspiring Minds’ (SWAYAM) is an integrated platform for offering online courses and covering school (9th to 12th) to Postgraduate Level.
- **SWAYAMPrabha:SWAYAMPrabhaisaninitiative** to provide 32 High Quality Educational Channels through DTH (Direct to Home) across the length and breadth of the country on a 24X7 basis.
- **National Digital Library (NDL):**The National Digital Library of India (NDL) is a project to build up a framework of virtual repository of learning resources with a single-window search facility. The**impact of “Digital India” on the Educational** **System,**a report by MHRD highlights that the government is planning to give**training to around** **100 million**people belonging to smaller villages and towns. The**intention behind these schemes is to boost** **ICT compatibility** with education and many more,

## **like**

## **Push for technology**

- Personalized learning through AI is another way to bridge the gap between skill and employability.
- It also aids better learning by treating each student as unique and adapting lessons accordingtohisorhercapacityandimproving learning outcomes by strengthening skilldevelopment.
- To help students to get access to multicultural multi-faceted learning while keeping the student’s engagement level high.

## **E-Kranti**

- A crucial success factor for rural education in India is the necessary infrastructural support for digitalization of education.
- Under ‘E-Kranti’, the government of India is trying to bridge the digital divide between remote and urban areas by providing basic infrastructural set-up for internet service.
- **Digital India**is expected to**decrease the** **unemployment ratio in the country.** Digital India also has the capability to bridge the skill gap which is arising due to the mismatch between the employers’ and prospective employee’s perspectives.
- **Issues related to illiteracy by furnishing** **education through digital platforms to children** **and teachers:** It provides an opportunity to access learning sources through a global platform, providing reachability to teachers and breaking the barriers to gain quality education through physical classes.
- The Indian educational system has evolved over the years. Technology is being utilized to reach the maximum number of people with a view to reduce digital illiteracy, rising opportunities in the field of educational providers, and providing uniform education to students in rural and urban areas. The goal of digital India is to bring urban and rural areas together for upliftment in Indian economy. **But there are certain challenges which are becoming** **obstacle to fulfil the intent behind Digital schemes in**

## **Education Sector**

- **Education now is in crisis especially because of** **thepandemic:schoolsandcollegescampuseswere**closed throughout the 2020 and now it has been extended in 2021.**According to ASER report,** In the time of pandemic, there are serious issues related to,
- Access, devices, content, curation, teachers, training, testing, exams, grades, funding, facilities, salaries, parents and fees.
- It is estimated that only about 25 percent of Indian households have an internet facility.
- For rural households, that number drops to 15 per cent. The worst affected, as always, will be the marginalized, rural and poor populations.
- Digital education is not about videos of lectures on blackboards by teachers on the internet. It is about appropriate platforms, technology, tools, interactivity, curation, content and a lot more. For this, India is still unprepared.
- Government schools and colleges do not have the resources to provide digital education.
- The **financial model for education**is falling apart everywhere during this pandemic. In India, the situation is even more complex because of the**lack of a proper policy** on digital education, infrastructure, and multiple languages.
- **Uninterrupted broadband connectivity is** **minimal:**According to**National Sample Survey** **data**for 2017-18, only 42 percent of urban and 15 percent of rural households had**internet access,** and only 34 per cent of urban and 11 per cent of rural persons had used the internet in the past 30 days.
- Anotherchallengeistokeepthousandsofchildren out of school when their parents return to their workspaces post lock down.
- Who will assume responsibility for a child’s safety and learning at home remains a huge problem is another concern for parents? Digital India is in its nascent stage. There is a long way to go before the maximum potential can be realized. SmartschoolsarebeingsetupindifferentpartsofIndia inaphased manner.Initially,acomputer labwhichhas facilities like broadband, projectors, white boards has been set up in most of the schools. The transition to set up smart classrooms in all the classes is happening gradually. **Digital India and the National ICT policy**has the potential for developing a knowledgeable, digital ecosystem in the education sector. It will play a key role in improving the Digital**Divide**in the country and integrating the education sector.**ANSWERS AND EXPLANATION** ---

## Question 112 (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2019-gs1-q112-unacademy**
**Q8. What makes the Indian society unique in sustaining its culture? Discuss. (150 words, 10 marks) Tag: Society of India- Diversity and culture.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about the** **spirit of Indian Society.**-**In Body,**-**Write how Indian society is unique in** **preserving its culture and not affected** **by homogenization.**-**In Conclusion, try to write about some** **challenges and the way forward.** **Answer:**India is a nation of great cultural diversity i.e, there are manydifferent types of socialgroupsand communities living here. These are communities defined by**cultural** **markers such as language, religion, sect, race, caste,** **even food habits, dress pattern,** etc. In western countries such diversities are lost or homogenised in the metropolitan culture but India has kept its identity through maintaining unity among diversified cultures.

## **Indian Society is Unique in Preserving its Culture**

## **Constitutional and legal provisions**

- Indian Constitution has a number **of** **provisions for the protection of minorities** **and cultural diversities. Article 29**of the Constitution states the protection of interests for minorities.**Article 30(1)** gives a provision to the minority communities to establish and administer an educational institute of their choice for protection of their culture and heritage.
- **Article 51** aims to promote harmony and the spirit of common brotherhood amongst all the people.
- Several government initiatives to promote the oneness of Indian society as well as encouraging multiple cultures. For example: **Ek Bharat Shreshtha Bharat** etc.
- Several laws protecting traditional laws of local communities like Forest rights act etc.
- **Movies, dramas, plays** - Certain Indian values have been cherished in the movies, dramas, plays, folk dances. Thus, creating psycho-emotive attachment to the society, belief and respect for one’s own value system. This is mostly missing in other parts of the world.
- Cultural tableaux on 15th August, 26th January creates a sense of fraternity along with making the young generation aware of rich cultural heritages.
- **Regional language**-**The Eighth Schedule of Indian** Constitution provides legal status to 22 languages and accordingly state and central governments donate funding to promote these languages. In India unlike western countries no language is anglicized. With unification and without losing diversity India has created a unique example in preserving its culture.
- InIndiareligiousandculturaltoleranceisreflected in the philosophy like **Sarva dharma sambhava,** **Service to humanity is service to God etc.** Unlike west countries, Indians do not sacrifice their own culture by pursuing any other culture.
- **Adaptability and spirituality help** India in maintaining its unique culture in the materialistic and homogenised world. The practice of yoga, meditation,charityetc.,aredeeplyrootedinIndian culture which prevents the negative impacts of westernisation and globalization.

## **Challenges in Preserving Culture**

- Withthegrowthofurbanisation,industrialisation **and globalization,** people are moving away from traditional cultural values towards materialistic and individualistic culture.
- **Religion, class and caste-based discriminations** are still frequent in the society. It can be seen in the forms of slums, gated communities, manual scavenging etc.
- The **ethnic, religious and region-based violence** are still prevalent in the country. For example: Muzaffar nagar riots, etc.
- With the growth of modern art forms and English language, some of the famous **art forms and** **languages are on the verge of extinction.**Indiansocietyhaspreserveditscultureinaveryunique way, though some sporadic incidents are greatly harming the unity and diversity of India. To address this, the government can focus more on proving the**resilience to Indian society and its composite, secular** **culture.**There should be more**people-to-people** **interaction in the forms of festivals, celebrations,** etc. It should be the prime objective of every citizen of India to cherish, promote, preserve the essence of Indian culture.
[Question ID: 2019-gs1-q112-unacademy]

## Question 114 (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2019-gs1-q114-unacademy**
**Q10. What are the challenges to our cultural practices in the name of secularism? (150 words, 10 marks) Tag: Society of India.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about the** **nature of Indian secularism.**-**In Body,**-**Write how secularism challenges various** **cultural practices.**-**Also, mention various positive changes** **done by secularism.**-**In Conclusion, try to write about overall** **significance secularism.**
**Answer:**Secularism refers to the**separation of religion from** **State.**In India all religions are treated as equal. State can intervene in all religions to ensure the freedom of individuals. The Indian Constitution**(Article 25- 28)** allows individuals the freedom to live by their religious beliefs and practices as they interpret these. In keeping with this idea of religious freedom for all, India also adopted a strategy of separating the power of religion and the power of the State.

## **Secularism Challenges Various Cultural Practices**

- Some critics argue that in the name of **secularism** **and modernity,** courts and government have been unnecessarily interfering in the religious and

## **cultural practices of India such as**

- Uttar Pradesh police ordered the companies in Noida to ensure employees from a particular community don’t offer their prayers in open public spaces, roads, parks.
- Tripura HC **banned animal sacrifice** in Tripura temples.
- SC has **allowed entry of women in Sabarimala** **Temple** and Haji Ali Dargah.
- Restriction on the height of Ganesh, duration of firecrackers, loudness of Navaratri mikes.
- It imposes one size fits all approach or uniformity onthepeopleinhighlydiversecountrieslikeIndia.
- The cultural practice and approach of personal laws is neglected.
- **Religion is a central part of people’s culture;** it cannot be completely separated in the name of secularism. Constitutional morality affects religious practices and belief of people.

## **Positive Changes Done by Secularism**

- In the name of religion and customs, many **evil**

## **practices are prevailing such as**

- Hindu daughters are deprived of equal property inheritance rights.
- Even after independence there were sporadic incidents of **Sati system.**-**Child marriages** are still practiced in certain parts of rural India.
- **Animals were sacrificed** in certain temples.
- Muslim women were subjected to the **practice** **of Triple talaq.**- Several**backward classes are prohibited in** **some local places** such as entering into the temples, using village ponds, using them for manual scavenging etc.
- Inter-caste marriage, Inter-gotra marriage were prohibited in khap panchayats.
- But because of various laws and judicial verdicts, one by one these regressive cultural practices have been outlawed by the Modern Secular Indian state.
- Equality, freedom, public order and right to life etc.arebothfundamentalandhumanrightswhich are crucial for the development of people.
- State should take steps for developing rational thinking and scientific temper among people.
- Culture cannot be stated, it should change as per the needs of society. Secularism has **created no challenge to any of our** **cultural practices**which are within the constitutional framework. Rather, secularism has only empowered the state and judiciary to challenge our ‘regressive’ cultural practices. In**Aruna Roy vs. The Union of** **India case, the Supreme Court**held that the concept**of secularism is not endangered if the basic tenets of** **all religions from**all over the world are studied and learned.**Value based education** will help the nation to fight against fanaticism, ill-will, violence, dishonesty and corruption. The state promotes greater secular values as a part of the secularization process. The concept of secularism is not merely a positive attitude of religious tolerance.
[Question ID: 2019-gs1-q114-unacademy]
---

---

## Question 115 (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2019-gs1-q115-unacademy**
**Q16. How is efficient and affordable urban mass transport key to the rapid economic development of India? (15 Marks, 250 words) Tag: Urbanization, their problems and their remedies.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about** **mass transport and its need.**-**In Body, briefly write how efficient urban** **mass transport is key for rapid economic** **development.**-**In Conclusion, try to mention the overall** **significance of urban mass transport.**
**Answer:** An urban transport system considers all elements of transportation, including the infrastructure, rolling stock, and traffic flows, in a city as a functioning entity. According to the 2011 census, around 31% of the population lives in urban areas. With the rapid growth ofurbanisation,themasstransportsystemisnecessary to serve public transport needs.

## **Urban Mass Transport is key for Rapid Economic Development**

<p align="center"><img src="images/unacademy_p467_img55.png" alt="Diagram" /></p>

- **Reduce congestion:** There is high concentration of human activities within a limited space, urban traffic congestion, especially at the commercial and business districts, is prevalent in all large cities worldwide. The use of public transport reduces the reliance of people on private automobiles.
- **Movement of Inputs and Outputs:** It facilitates the movements of different inputs like raw materials, fuels, machineries etc. and outputs for the smooth functioning of the entire production & supply system of the country.
- **Labour Mobility:** It facilitates the mobility of labour for expansion of industries between different regions of the country. It opens up gainful employment opportunities to unemployed and under-employed labourers of the country.
- **Facilitates Specialization:** It facilitates clearcut geographical specialisation in respect of productionbydevelopingtheextentofthemarket.
- ExtensionandPerfectionofMarkets:Itextendsthe sizeofmarketofvariousproductsofthecountryto distantareasandremovesallmarketimperfections smoothly and efficiently, by establishing a link between production centres, distribution centres and ultimate consumers to the transport system.
- **Connect inaccessible regions:** It makes way for tapping vast unexplored land resources of urban areas and its peripheral regions. It leads in effective and efficient infrastructure management in urban regions.
- **Widening Outlook of the People:** It enriches the social, cultural and political outlook of our people. It also removes conservative attitudes, superstition and ignorance of people.
- **Quality of life:** short commutation enhances productivity by providing adequate leisure time for people. It also reduces pollution by reducing the number of private vehicles on roads.
- It ensures public safety by reducing road accidents. **Careful planning of the urban goods movement** **is necessary to ensure the economic vitality of the** **urban economy on the one hand and to contribute** **to sustainable urban mass transport on the other.** Consideration of road safety in urban planning and development of transport policy is also important. It can cater the need of growing urbanisation and enhance growth of the cities which contributes around three-fourth part of GDP.

## Question 116 (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2019-gs1-q116-unacademy**
**Q18. Do we have cultural pockets of small India all over the nation? Elaborate with examples. (15 Marks, 250 words) Tag: Society of India.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about the** **diversity of India and culture.**-**In Body,**-**Write briefly about the diversity of India.**-**Write how small pockets of India are** **there all over the nation and mention** **some issues with it.**-**In Conclusion, try to link the importance of** **cultural diversity.** **Answer:** **Culture is used to relate language, ideas, customs,** **and social behaviour of a society.**India is a diverse nation and home to several communities, which interchanged traditions among themselves. Despite thediversitythesenseoftogethernessisdevelopeddue to**peaceful coexistence.** The development of transport and communication helped in bringing small India together in the forms of different pockets.

## **Diversity in Different Cultural Pockets of India**

- **Linguistic diversity:** Officially there are 122 languages but 22 languages in the Eighth Schedule of the Constitution give cultural pockets like Assamese, Gujarati, Konkani, Maithili, Manipuri, Oriya, Tamil, Telugu etc.
- **Religious diversity:** According to the data on Population by Religious Communities of Census 2011, Hindu 96.63 crores (79.8%); Muslim 17.22 crores (14.2%); Christian 2.78 crores (2.3%); Sikh 2.08 crores (1.7%); Buddhist 0.84 crores (0.7%); Jain 0.45 crores (0.4%) are dispersed all over the nation forming cultural pockets.
- **Racial diversity:** Anthropologists categorize Indians as belonging to racial admixture. Mongoloids are largely confined to the Northeastern region whereas Negritos are found on the Andaman Islands. Because of this, India has been termed as an ethnological museum.
- A particular ethnic group shared a common culture, common language or dialect, a common religion, a common norm, practices, customs and history. Such multiple groups appeared as cultural pockets.

## **Small India in Different Cultural Pockets**

- **Thedevelopmentofcommunicationandtransport** facilitated industrialisation and urbanisation. The people are in search of better opportunities in terms of education, employment, living conditions etc. migrated from one place to another place.
- The like Delhi, Mumbai, **metropolitan cities** Bangalore etc. became key attractions for people. At present these cities are home of people with different religion, culture, language, race etc.
- A picture of small India can be seen in these cities in the form of cultural pockets.

## **Challenges due to Different Cultural Pockets of India**

- The People’s Linguistic Survey of India **identified** **780 languages of which 50 got extinct** in the past five decades.
- There are frequent incidents of **attacks on people** **with different cultures** in these pockets.
- In some regions there is a **growing sense of** **regionalism and protectionism**which is opposing people from outsiders. India has a unique and distinct place among the other countries of the world as a host of a culture that has been prevalent for a long time. It is perhaps**one of the** **most diverse countries in the world.**The government steps like**Ek Bharat Shreshtha Bharat** can go a long way in celebrating and strengthening this diversity.

## Question 117 (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2019-gs1-q117-unacademy**
**Q19. What are the continued challenges for women in India against time and space? (15 Marks, 250 words) Tag: Society of India.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about the** **position of women in Indian society.**-**In Body, write what are the challenges faced** **by women in both time and space.**-**In Conclusion, try to assess the situation of** **women and suggest a way forward.** **Answer:** **Women constitute around 48% of the population**of India, still they face multiple challenges. According to**UNDP Gender Inequality (2019), India’s maternal** **mortality rate is over 174 deaths per 1 lakh live births,**percentage of**women in parliament is just around** **14.5%**and there are growing**incidents of crimes and** **its severity against women.**

## **Challenges Faced by Women in Time**

- **Ancient and mediaeval times:** There was lack of participation of women in the decision making of Sabha and Simitis.
- The practice like polygamy, polyandry etc. were prevalent and women did not given share in the property and treated as “paraya dhan”.

## **Modern times**

- **Preference of son**– Economic survey quoted**‘Son meta preference’, the**phenomena when for the desire of having a son couple continue to produce children until a son is born. The Economic Survey has mentioned that the desire for a male child has created 21 million**“unwanted” girls in India.**-**Health & Education:** Health and education are widely neglected and compromised. Girls are not provided with adequate nutrition and withdrawn from the education system at an early age for marriage.
- **Female infanticide:** Even though the practice isoutlawed,300,000to600,000femalefoetuses are aborted every year in India because of the preference for boys, according to a 2011 study by The Lancet.
- **Hygiene:** There is a lack of adequate sanitation facilities in India. Women are compelled to use open defecation and abuse during this is also a serious threat to them.
- **Domestic crimes:** Domestic violence, dowry, harassment, abuse, demolishing self-dignity such as triple talaq and crime at public places are frequent in India.
- **Lack of representation and power:** The women are not represented adequately in the Parliament and other administrative bodies and do not have adequate power and share in policy making and implementation.
- **Poverty and feminization of old age is** **emerging in the present time.** There is also a dual burden on women of work and family care.

## **Challenges Faced by Women in Space**

- In urban areas women face various issues like **crimes, sanitation, burden of care economy,** glass ceiling, unequal wages etc.
- In rural areas there is **skewed sex ratio due to** **migration**of males, thus women are facing**feminisation of agriculture,** burden of patriarch and poverty at same time.
- In certain south Indian states women are more empowered in comparison to certain northern states. Some southern states also show good indicators in parameters like education, health, etc. Women empowerment is **critical for achieving** **sustainable development**and growth of the country. There is some improvement in the position of women due the**Constitutional, Legislative and** **Administrative safeguards**but still there is a lot more to be done for gender parity. Women empowerment cannot be possible unless women self-empower themselves. There is a need to formulate**reducing** **feminized poverty, promoting education of women,** and prevention and elimination of violence against women to build New and empowered India.
[Question ID: 2019-gs1-q117-unacademy]
---

## Question 118 (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2019-gs1-q118-unacademy**
**Q20. Are we losing our local identity for the global identity? Discuss. (15 Marks, 250 words) Tag: Society of India.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about** **globalization and Indian society.**-**In Body,**-**Write how we are losing local identities** **for global identities with examples.**-**Also write how local identities are still** **preserved in the era of globalization.**-**In Conclusion, try to write a balanced view** **with a tilt in favour of the resilience of** **Indian culture.** **Answer:**Globalization refers to the**growing interdependence** **among different people, regions and countries in the** **world.**India was not isolated from the world even two thousand years ago. Famous**trade routes like the Silk** **Road**connected India to the great civilisations, which existed in China, Persia, Egypt and Rome. At present, there is a**growing sense of insecurity** among people for losing cultural identities under the influence of globalization.

## **Losing Local Identities for Global Identities**

- The Indian **family structure** is moving from the joint family towards the nuclear family under the influence of globalization in the metropolitan cities.
- There is a change in the **food culture** with the emergence of globalization. Global food brands like KFC, McDonalds, Dominos etc. are famous in Indian market.
- The **clothing system** has also changed with the influence of global brands, jeans, t-shirts etc. becameaninseparablepartnowandthetraditional Indian handloom and khadi became marginal.
- The use of **English language** as a means of communication and administrative work also depicts the influence of western culture. The large number of tribal/local languages are endangered or vanished due to less use & awareness.
- With the growth of a large number of MNCs a **new** **corporate culture** which promotes individualism emerged in India, which is very different from the traditional agrarian culture which celebrated community relations. It employed a large number of young populations with its lucrative offers and monetary incentives.
- **The Indian Constitution, administrative,** **legislative and judicial system** is also borrowed from various countries and modified as per the need and conditions of India.

## **Local Identities Still Preserved in Globalization**

- Despite wide use of English language, **linguistic** **and regional identities** of people are still intact which is evident with the statehood demand by various linguistic communities and regions like Haryana, Andhra Pradesh etc.
- The **regional festivals, traditions and rituals** still play an important role in the lives of people and these are also popularised in other countries like Diwali, Holi etc.
- The **Indian cuisine, spices and cooking methods** are accepted by many countries of the world like Dosa, Idli etc.
- **TheIndianmovies,TVserialsetc.areverypopular** among the people of other countries, which spread Indian culture in the world.
- **The Indian handlooms, khadi ware, tribal** **handicrafts** and artifacts have a huge market in various countries all over the world.
- **The Indian traditional healing process, yoga,** **meditation**etc. are accepted by the world. Celebration of**International Yoga Day**is a very recent example of it. Globalizationhasawideroletoplayworldwideandleft its footprints at every sphere of life. The**interchange** **of world views, culture and ideas has resulted in** **a major transformation of the lifestyle**and living standard of people globally. Indian culture is no bar to this transformation process.**Despite the negative** **impact our deep-rooted traditions and customs** **have not loosened up their hold with the emergence** **of globalization,**but**it is transforming itself**and emerging with new identities with pride throughout the world.**ANSWERS AND EXPLANATION** ---
[Question ID: 2019-gs1-q118-unacademy]

## Question 119 (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2018-gs1-q119-unacademy**
**Q8. “Caste system is assuming new identities and associational forms. Hence, caste system cannot be eradicated in India.” Comment. (10 marks, 150 words) Tag: Salient features of Indian Society.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, start with the definition of** **caste.**-**In Body, elaborate how the caste system is** **takingnewidentitiesandformsandmention** **where it is irrelevant.**-**Conclude with stating the current scenario** **and a positive way to eradicate it.** **Answer:**Caste is that**intense form of social class organization**in which the status of individuals in the hierarchy is**defined by descent and birth.** While caste might not inevitablyplaythesameroleitdidintheoldendays,its shifting character ensures it has adapted to play a role in contemporary society.

## **New identities and associational forms**

- **Sanskritization** is where the lower castes seek upward mobility by emulating the rituals and practices of the upper or dominant castes. It is increasing day by day.
- This aspiration points out that caste awareness, an implicit awareness about **apparent Brahmin** **supremacy,** and a corollary of other castes’ lower standing exist.
- Caste has penetrated the **political arena,** with castes representing easily identifiable vote banks.
- Castewaspreferredovertheclassascasteidentities are much more apparent than class, and almost everyone can state which caste they belong to.
- Through politics, it has flowed into **education** as well,intheformofreservationforthelowercastes.
- This web of caste influence finishes a complete circle with reservations in jobs as well for the Scheduled Castes.
- **Development policies** targeting the backward castesandscheduledhavebenefittedonlyasection of the population. These sections have emerged as the elite, and this has created a division within the backwards castes.

## **It is becoming irrelevant in some area**

- The most critical rule among the caste regarding **puritywascommensalityorregulationsregarding** eating and drinking with or receiving food and water from other castes.
- Studentsofdifferentcastes,unawareofcommensal rules, freely ate with each other in school and at large public gatherings, i.e., marriages, inter-caste mingling happened without commensal rules being adhered to.
- Inter-caste marriages have become relatively common in urban areas and cities.
- Marriage is also a result of the free choice of two partners, the role of caste in determining the match is declining. In its older forms, caste has become irrelevant, but rather than getting replaced with a class-based society, the **caste system has taken shelter in other walks of** **our social life.**The caste system in India is bearing changes due to progress in education, technology, modernization and changes in general social outlook. However,despitethegeneralimprovementinsituations of the lower castes, India has still a**long way to go, to** **root out the evils of the caste system** from society.
[Question ID: 2018-gs1-q119-unacademy]

## Question 120 (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2018-gs1-q120-unacademy**
**Q9. ‘Despite implementation of various programmes for eradication of poverty by the government in India, poverty is still existing’. Explain by giving reasons. (10 marks, 150 words) Tag: Poverty and Developmental Issues.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **InIntroduction,trytostartwithhowpoverty** **is a major challenge and despite showing a** **decreasing trend with the help of various** **schemes, it still exists.**-**In Body, elaborate the reasons for the** **continuance of poverty even after so many** **programmes and schemes.**-**In Conclusion, mention the potential of** **MGNREGA and Garib Kalyan Rozgar** **Abhiyan (GKRA) to reduce poverty.** **Answer:**Eradication of poverty remains a**major challenge** **of planned economic development.**After the implementation of the**1991 economic reforms,**India emerged as a success story of globalization. While on the one hand, India can sustain its rapid economic growth, on the other, India is struggling to provide basic services and infrastructures to its population. India continues to have a large population, which is certainly not being able to afford basic things. This is, despite multidimensional poverty almost halved between 2005-06 and 2015-16, climbing down to 27.5%. But still, poverty exists, even with the various schemes and policies.**Reasons for the continuance of poverty even after so**

## **many programmes and schemes**

- **Problems with the poverty eradication** **programme by the government:** Each poverty alleviation scheme such as MGNREGA, Public Distribution Systems (PDS), Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT) and other Centrally Sponsored Scheme suffer from an array of

## **difficulties such as**

- Fiscal crisis faced by State Governments resulting in lower spending on social sectors.
- Failure of Targeted Public Distribution System (TPDS) to reach the poorest in northern and eastern states.
- Non-adaptability to local conditions.
- Late disbursement of funds.
- Reallocation of funds to unrelated recurring expenditure.
- Wide-ranging rent-seeking practices.
- **Effects of COVID-19, informal economy, and** **unemployment:** **The ongoing COVID-19 pandemic as a public** **health catastrophe has affected all ways of life.** **But lockdown has pushed more people towards**

## **poverty**

- The effects of the COVID-19 crisis on lowskilled migrant labourers and informal workers have been overwhelming.
- Early evidence suggests that there has been a massive increase in unemployment and an equally dramatic fall in earnings.
- During the lockdown period in the current pandemic, many labourers lost their jobs and due to lack of social security nets and formal benefits, travelled back to their home without any guarantee of returning.
- As per the International Labour Organization (ILO), 400 million workers from India’s informal sector are likely to be pushed deeper into poverty due to COVID-19. To reduce the hardship of people affected by poverty and to give relief to the informal economy, the Indian government has launched **Garib Kalyan Rozgar** **Abhiyan(GKRA).Goingforward,theimplementation** capacity of States, particularly across the poorest districts, will play a crucial role in determining the success of MNREGA and GKRA schemes. Furthermore, India needs to ramp up MGNREGA, introduce a guaranteed urban employment scheme, and boost further cash transfers to poor households.

## Question 121 (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2018-gs1-q121-unacademy**
**Q10.HowistheIndianconceptofsecularismdifferent from the western model of secularism? Discuss. (10 marks, 150 words) Tag: Secularism.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to define both western** **and Indian secularism.**-**In Body, compare the Indian concept of** **secularism and the western model of** **secularism.**-**Conclude with the significance of secularism** **in a modern democracy.**
**Answer:**The idea of secularism was incorporated in the Indian Constitution by the 42nd Constitutional Amendment, which amended the Preamble, and declared India a secular nation. Secularism is articulated over the years throughout the political process embedded in the notions of equality and democracy, while the western concept of secularism denies religion any space in the functioning of the state. Western dictionaries define secularism as a**separation of religion from the state,**butIndiansecularismmeanstheprofusionofreligion. Indian secularism trips with western secularism in some aspects, but still, there exist fundamental differences.**Difference between the Indian concept of secularism** **and the western model of secularism:** **WESTERN MODEL OF SECULARISM** **INDIAN MODEL OF SECULARISM**The State is**separate**from the functioning of all The state is**neutral**to all religious groups but**not**religious institutions and groups. Believes in**total non-interference**of religion.**necessarily separate.**The principle of secularism lies in forming a**positive** **relationship**between the state and religion. Does not believe in an open display of religion with All expressions of religion are substantiated equally an exception for places of worship. Laws are made in isolation from religious principles. The state**cannot give any financial support**to educationalinstitutionsrunbyreligiouscommunities. A**single uniform code of law**is used to dispense justice regardless of religious background. The State**does not intervene in the affairs of religion**till the time religion is working within the limits of the law. Example: The western model prohibits any public policy to be drafted based on religion; therefore, the state is absolutely distanced from the religious activities and practices of its citizens. Thus, Indian secularism practised in a distinct form because of an interaction between what already existed in a society with religious diversity and the ideas that came from the west. It resulted in**an equal focus on** **intra-religious and inter-religious domination.**It with support from the state. The law seeks to welcome the multiple religious principles that followers of different religions adhere to. The Indian Constitution**sanctions partial financial** **support for religious schools**and the**funding of** **religious buildings**and**infrastructure by the state.**The law is the equivalent to all citizens; specific personal laws regarding marriage and property rights are**different for every community.**However, they all yielded equal consideration under the**Indian Penal** **Code.**The state**shall interfere in religion to remove evils** **in it.** Example:Indiahasintervenedbyenforcinglegislation against the practices of sati or widow-burning, dowry, animal and bird sacrifice, child marriage, and preventing Dalits from entering temples equally resisted the oppression of Dalits and women within Hinduism, the discrimination against women within Indian Islam or Christianity, and the possible threats that a majority community might pose to the rights of the minority religious communities.
[Question ID: 2018-gs1-q121-unacademy]

## Question 122 (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2018-gs1-q122-unacademy**
**Q17. Mention core strategies for the transformation of aspirational districts in India and explain the nature of convergence, collaboration and competitionforitssuccess.(15marks,150words) Tag: Urbanization, their problems, and their remedies.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to write about the** **‘Transformation of Aspirational Districts’** **programme.**-**In Body, write about core strategies for the** **transformation of aspirational districts** **in India and the nature of convergence,** **collaboration,andcompetitioncomponents.**-**Conclude with stating how it showcases the** **spirit of cooperative federalism.** **Answer:**India is on a**high economic growth trajectory.**For the uniform growth pattern, the**enhancement of the** **Human Development Index**and reduction in the significant inter-state and inter-district variations in development is required. For this to happen, focused interventions were necessary where the need was greatest, and the**‘Transformation of Aspirational** **Districts’ programme was launched in this backdrop** **in January 2018.**The ‘Transformation of Aspirational Districts’ Programme**aims to expeditiously improve** **the socio-economic status of 117 districts from across** **28 states.** **The Broad Contours of the Strategy Envisaged under**

## **this Programme**

- **Addressing negativity by making development a** **mass movement: In the programme architecture,**

## **the following elements address this issue**

- The ‘backward districts’ to be called **‘Aspirational Districts.’**- District officials should**draw up a Vision and** **Action Plan** through public participation, to ensure that their district is a full-fledged partner in creating a New India.
- **Identification of Champions of Change at District** **andRegionalLevelstoMakeDevelopmentaMass**

## **Movement**

- TheschemearchitectureencouragestheStates **and District administration** to give a lead role to people from all walks of life especially those who have a track record of effecting change despite existing challenges.
- **Convergence of Initiatives of the Central and**

## **State Governments**

- A major approach under this programme is **to ensure convergence between** **different government schemes** and to seek complementarity between public initiatives and the private efforts of a household.

## **States are the Main Drivers**

- Senior Government of India officials at the rank of Joint Secretary/Additional Secretary have been appointed as ‘Guardians’ (or ‘Prabharis’ in Hindi) for a district.
- The Prabhari Officers appointed under this programme are from the same State cadre. Their role then becomes to **act as a bridge** **between** the Central and State Governments.
- **Evidence-based** **Decision-making** **and**

## **Competition Among Districts**

- To ascertain the availability of resources for each sector through different sources, assess the critical gaps, and set periodic targets to achieve them.
- To rank the performance of districts by capturing the percentage change in key indicators on a monthly or quarterly basis and put these numbers in public domain like Delta ranking.

## **Funding for the Programme**

- Funds and other resources made available by the private sector under Corporate Social Responsibility, and some local funds like the District Mineral Funds are to be utilized for critical gap funding. Key Central Ministries have also prioritized these districts in their respective programmes. Thus, the programme emphasizes on a **focused** **approachtoenhancetheperformanceacrosshealthand**nutrition, education, agriculture and water resources, financial inclusion and skill development, and basic infrastructure. The Aspirational Districts Programme**intends to turn development into a mass movement,**facilitated by Governments at different levels: Centre, State and District. Seen from this perspective, the architecture of this programme**showcases the spirit of** **cooperative federalism in India.** ---

## Question 123 (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2018-gs1-q123-unacademy**
**Q18. ‘Women’s movement in India has not addressed the issues of women of lower social strata.’ Substantiate your view. (15 marks, 150 words) Tag: Role of women and women’s organization.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to write briefly about the** **condition of women in India.**-**In Body, elaborate how Women’s Movement** **in India has not addressed the issues of** **women of lower social strata.**-**Conclude with mentioning government** **schemes to address this issue.** **Answer:**The status**of women**in India has gone under**several**changes from post-independence. The women’s movement in India**gained prominence in the 1970s.**However, the scenario of women in lower social strata, especially from**vulnerable groups still face** **exclusions.**Though the**Constitution of India has** **enshrined several provisions (Article 16, Article 17,** **Article 46, etc.)**for Dalit communities to safeguard and promote their socio-economic status to bring them back in the mainstream of population, the exclusionary mechanism of entrenched hierarchical social relations has brought differential outcomes for women belonging to vulnerable groups.**Conditions of Women of Lower Social Strata**

## **Politically, Economically and Socially**

- **Women of lower social strata**have to bear the triple burden of**deprivation in terms of caste,** **gender and poverty.**-**Women of lower social strata**are forced to**enter** **the labour market at a very early age** in order to sustain their families. Traditionally allocated menial and stigmatized works are generally assigned to them.
- Better paid and dignified jobs continue to be out of reach for them through a systematic denial of rights to higher education, skill training,assetsandotherproductiveresources.
- This process of systematic denial further ensures their exclusion from socio-political scenarios and keeps them **restricted to the** **bottom of the society as invisible citizens.**- For women of lower strata, they are coerced to**be** **victimized in the patriarchy.**- Particularly, Dalit and Tribal women are bearing the burden of**double-day caste and** **the division of labor based on sex.**- They are**demeaned and degraded,** and their body is a free terrain of colonization by men from other communities.
- Women of lower socialstrata are at **thelowest level** **of economic and educational structures.** - They are poor, illiterate, sexually harassed, face caste violence and exploited.
- Doubly,triply,ormultiplydiscriminated,these women face a lot of struggles daily; otherwise just being overwhelmed by those surges of discrimination up to them.
- After Independence, **neither the women** **organizations nor activists addressed the caste** **and gender intersectionality.**- Dalit movement became**‘masculinized’ and** **women activists started treating women as a** **homogenous group.**- Women’s lives in India and women belonging to lower social strata in particular are circumscribed by what can be termed as five ‘P’s:**Patriarchy,** **Productive resources access inadequacy, Poverty,** **Promotion advancement insufficiency and** **Powerlessness.**- For women, especially those from marginalized communities, it is getting increasingly difficult to access the economic resources due to**biased** **customary laws and patriarchal norms,**and is perpetuated by gender blind policies which have exacerbated the obstacles that women confront and made them vulnerable to**food insecurity—** **malnutrition, chronic hunger and starvation.**-**Political Participation and Empowerment:**Women from lower strata are**politically** **marginalized,**but rural women especially Dalits and tribals are**given even less of a voice** in the decision-making process.
- The reservation system is in force for SCs/STs tohaveseatsinthelocalpanchayatortoprotect their rights (town assembly). But the role being played by SC/ST women is **consistently** **subordinated to their male counterparts.** - SC/ST women who attempt to utilize their power in the panchayat are met with male and dominant caste backlash, pressure and sometimes violence.
- Many a times Dalit women are told they are not even allowed to sit on a chair but must take their place on the floor. The achievement of equal status for women was one of the specific **objectives implicit in the Preamble,**Fundamental Rights and Directive Principles of the State Policy in the Constitution of India. Government schemes like**Beti Bachao Beti Padhao, Dhanlaxmi** **scheme are small steps in improving sex ratio and** **education for women.**But there is a need for**the** **promulgation of the provision as an untouchable** **free country through a constructive plan of action** **effectively.** The discriminatory laws, traditional practices and dogmatism against lower strata women should be abolished by providing them the social security for their rehabilitation.
[Question ID: 2018-gs1-q123-unacademy]
---

---

## Question 124 (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2018-gs1-q124-unacademy**
**Q19. ‘Globalization is generally said to promote cultural homogenization but due to these cultural specificities appear to be strengthened in the Indian society.’ Elucidate. (15 marks, 150 words) Tag: Effects of globalization on Indian society.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to define Globalization** **and how it helps to promote Cultural** **Homogenization.**-**In Body, mention the points suggesting that** **globalization has strengthened cultural** **specificities in Indian Society.**-**Conclude with showing how globalization** **has the potential to create a new culture to** **strengthen the Indian society.** **Answer:**Globalization can be**depicted as increasing global** **interconnectedness.**It is a**process rather than** **an outcome,**which refers to the**trend toward the** **growing interconnectedness of different parts of the** **world,nottotheirbeinginterconnected.Itprimarilyis**an**interchange of economic, social, cultural, political,** **technological attributes**that takes place between societies when different societies meet each other.**Evidence Suggesting that Globalization has**

## **strengthened Cultural Specificities in Indian Society**

- A central assertion is that all cultures will become similar, that is, homogeneous. Others argue that there is a growing tendency towards glocalisation of culture.
- **Glocalization applies to the mixing of the** **global with the local.**-**It is not entirely spontaneous, nor is it wholly** **delinked from the commercial interests of** **globalisation.**-**It is a strategy often utilised by foreign firms** **while dealing with local traditions to enhance** **their marketability.**- InIndia,alltheforeigntelevisionchannelslike Star, MTV, Channel V and Cartoon Network**use Indian languages.**- Even McDonald’s sells**only vegetarian and** **chicken products in India** and not its beef products, which are popular abroad.
- McDonald’s goes vegetarian during the Navratri festival.
- In the **field of music,** one can see the growth of popularity of ‘Bhangra pop’, ‘Indi pop’, fusion music and even remixes.
- Cultural consumption is playing a crucial role in the process of globalization especially in shaping the **growth of cities.**- Presently, cultural consumption (of**art, food,** **fashion, music, tourism)** shapes to a large extent the growth of cities.
- This is evident in the spurt in the **growth of** **shopping malls, multiplex cinema halls,** **amusement** parks and ‘water world’ in every major city in India.
- Most significantly **advertisements and the** **media in general promote a culture**where spending is important.**TheintensityofIndianculturehasbeenitsopen-ended** **approach.**Throughout the modern period, reformers and nationalists**actively debated tradition and** **culture.**Culture cannot be viewed as an**unchanging** **fixed entity**that can either collapse or persist the same when faced with social change. What is more expected even today, is that**globalization will take the lead to** **the formation of not just new local traditions but** **global ones too,**while the cultural specificities in the Indian society**will continue to remain strengthened.** ---

## Question 125 (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2018-gs1-q125-unacademy**
**Q20. ‘Communalism arises either due to power struggle or relative deprivation.’ Argue by giving suitable illustrations. (15 marks, 150 words) Tag: Communalism.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to define Communalism** **and how it is related to politics.**-**In Body, elaborate whether Communalism** **ariseseitherduetopowerstruggleorrelative** **deprivation.**-**Conclude with how Communalism ideology** **can be minimized.** **Answer:**Communalism is a**political doctrine which makes** **use of religio-cultural differences to achieve** **political ends.**When, on the basis of religio-cultural differences, a community initiates political demands deliberately, then communal awareness turns into communalism. Communalism as a concept emerged since members of a multi religious society had to witness and confront**the behavioral pattern**practiced by specific communities per se.**There may be several** **factorsthatmay beattributed to thecauseand growth** **of Communalism in India.**

## **Communalism Arising due to Power Struggle**

- **Stagnant economy during the British Rule:** The stagnation of economy may have affected the aspirations and economic prosperity for certain sections within society.
- Certain sections of society, usually termed as **‘Middle Class’,**used communalism as a weapon for their survival at the cost of other classes in society. Consequently, other leaders from the community and political parties joined to**fuel the tension of Communalism in** **India.**- Itmaybewellillustratedwiththeemergenceof modern politics with**its roots in the partition** **of Bengal in 1905 and thefeature of a separate** **electorate under the Government of India** **Act, 1909.**- Later, British government also alleviated various communities**through Communal** **award in 1932** that endorsed strong resistance from Gandhiji and others.
- All these acts were done by the British government to appease one section of the community over other communities, for their own political needs.
- This feeling of communalism **has deepened** **since then, fragmenting the Indian society** **and being a cause of unrest.**- In some States it was seen that**political** **competition became a cause for violence**where governments that were**not dependent** **on minority support** saw prolonged and intense rioting and attacks on minorities.
- Unleashing of what are seen as **“institutionalized riot systems”**with rumor mongering, recruitment of participants and other provocative activities; and, the existence of patronage networks with their processes of give-and-take, were some other explanations of communal riots. For instance,**U.P. in the** **1990s went through political instability** with the erosion of a single party system.
- The State also remained mired in caste and communal politics.
- The consequence was that localized tensions and skirmishes acquired serious dimensions leading to large-scale rioting with extra-local implications in the 2000s.

## **Communalism Arising due to Relative Deprivation**

- **Deprivation makes different ethnic groups fight** **for limited resources** which leads to the concept of insider vs outsider.
- For example, Case with **Assamese and** **Non-Assamese**and**Gorkhas and Bengalis.** Deprivation of the native groups led to movements throughout the state.
- The **progress of one community is perceived** **with unpleasantness and the economic collapse** **of the opposite side** is eagerly welcomed in a communally charged environment. The vested interest groups having economic and other antisocial motivations trigger communal conflicts in order to gain through a riot.
- Deprivationmakesiteasiertomanipulateagroup. Rather than self-introspect they accuse others for their situation. It affects them to support anyone who can help them marginally move above their misery. Communalism is an ideology which preaches that communities are formed **on religious lines.**Members of a community share common interests which are incompatible with the interests of other religious communities. In certain**political contexts,** **communalism can lead to communal violence.**A**processofde-communalizationneedstobeestablished**at every level of governance to raise awareness and spread social harmony.**The role of education and** **media**can go a long way in achieving it.**ANSWERS AND EXPLANATION** ---
[Question ID: 2018-gs1-q125-unacademy]

## Question 126 (Year: 2017 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2017-gs1-q126-unacademy**
**Q9. In the context of the diversity of India, can it be said that the regions form cultural units rather than the States? Give reasons with examples for your viewpoint. (150 words, 10 marks) Tag: Society of India.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about** **India’s cultural diversity.**-**In Body,**-**Write How regions form cultural units** **rather than states.**-**Also, write how states form cultural** **units.**-**In Conclusion, try to mention a balanced** **view.** **Answer:** India is a highly diverse country in terms of language, religion, culture, race etc. It is very unique because such a large number of populations of the country retaineditsdiversityfromancienttimetillpresent.The endless varieties of physical and cultural features prove that India is the epitome of the world.

## **Regions forms Cultural Units**

- Along with contacts with outside cultures, cultural exchange between different regions of India has also continued. Our cultural identities are based on various factors such as religion and region.
- Regionalidentitiesaremorereal.Peopleofdifferent States may have a common regional culture. For example: Despite different States in South India, Idli, Dosa and other similar foods are found in the entire region.
- In Bengal, both Hindus and Muslims take pride in being Bengalis. Elsewhere one finds Hindus, Christians and Muslims sharing several elements of regional culture.
- Ethnic culture is solid among the tribal groups. For example, in the small state of Nagaland, there are more than a dozen tribes who diverge from each other in their dress, speech and beliefs. In Chhattisgarh,theBastardistricthasseveralgroups claiming different ethnic origins.
- The regions in the North, South, East and West of India have their characteristic cultures, yet these did not develop in absolute isolation. Rice is a staple diet in Eastern and Southern India and wheat is more used in North India.
- Even after the division of Madhya Pradesh, People of Chattisgarh have the same sense of affinity with people of Madhya Pradesh.
- Despite physical barriers, Indians travelled from one part of the country to another for trade or pilgrimage. Some regions were united together through conquests or by the alliance.

## **States forms Cultural Units**

- Culture varies from place to place and country to country. Its development is based on the historical process operating in a local, regional or national context.
- In India language, race etc. also influenced formation of States apart from administrative convenience. These claim a different identity and culture from the rest of the people. For example: Formation of Tamil Nadu and Andhra Pradesh over language claim different cultures.
- The state of Maharashtra has different cultural practices and traditions from adjoining Goa and Gujrat. The composite nature of our culture is reflected in our music, dance forms, drama and art forms like paintings, sculpture and architecture as well, it cannot be reflected in a region or state. The beauty of India’s diversity is tolerance and coexistence of different cultures. Despite geographical diversity and climatic variations India experiences an inherent unity. This is the reason that the civilizations got destroyed while Indian culture is present till today because the basis of development was spirituality and not materialism.
[Question ID: 2017-gs1-q126-unacademy]

## Question 127 (Year: 2017 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2017-gs1-q127-unacademy**
**Q10. What are the two major legal initiatives by the State since Independence addressing discrimination against Scheduled Tribes (STs)? (150 words, 10 marks) Tag: Society of India.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about the** **schedule caste and various discriminations.**-**In Body,**-**Write major legal initiatives by the** **State since Independence to address** **discrimination.**-**In Conclusion, try to write about the** **significance of initiatives.** **Answer:**The framers of the Constitution took note of the fact that certain communities in the country were suffering from extreme social, educational and economic backwardness on account of the primitive agricultural practices, lack of infrastructure facilities and geographical isolation.**The Constitution of India** **in Article 366 (25) prescribes that the Scheduled** **Tribes means such tribes or tribal communities as are** **deemed under Article 342 of the Constitution to be** **Scheduled Tribes.**-**Major Legal Initiatives taken by the State** **Addressing Discrimination Against STs:** **Reservation:Article17undertheIndianConstitution,** prohibits discrimination and obligate states to make laws to protect STs. The government provides for reservation of STs in jobs and educational institutions, for upliftment and overall development of people.
- **In the elections of Panchayat, State legislature** **and Parliament also, seats are reserved for their** **proper representation.** **Forest Rights Act:** It provides for recognition of the rights of communities to govern, use, and conserve forests they have traditionally managed and utilised, reversing the colonial and postcolonial history in which the state had taken over control of forests.
- It granted **legal recognition to the rights** of traditional forest dwelling communities, partially correcting the injustice caused by the forest laws.
- It provides guidelines for **undertaking** **developmental facilities** of the villages in and around forested areas.
- It empowers and encourageslocal **self-governance** **amongst** the marginalised tribal communities and forest dwellers. The laws assume importance in a developing economy like India. These provide a redressal mechanism for vulnerable and marginalised communities and groups. For the necessary evil of development and infrastructural growth, safeguarding their traditions, heritage and identity that forms an important part of cultural diversity.

## Question 128 (Year: 2017 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2017-gs1-q128-unacademy**
**Q 11. The spirit of tolerance and love is not only an interesting feature of Indian society from very early times, but it is also playing an important part at the present. Elaborate. (250 words, 15 marks) Tag: Society of India.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to briefly write about the** **love and tolerance in Indian society.**-**In Body,**-**Write how tolerance and love is a feature** **of Indian Society from an early time.**-**Also, mention its importance in the** **present.**-**In Conclusion, try to mention the overall** **significance of such values.** **Answer:**Tolerance is an important concept that helps people to live together peacefully. It means that you accept other people’s opinions and preferences, even when they live in a way that you don’t agree with. Society defines love as something that selfishness satisfies your own desires. India being a multicultural society, both love and tolerance have been intrinsic features of society since ancient times.**Tolerance and Love is Feature of Indian Society from**

## **Early Time**

- AnessentialaspectofIndiancultureistolerance.In India, tolerance and liberalismare embedded in all religions, castes, communities, etc. Many foreign cultures invaded India, and Indian society yielded every culture the opportunity of prospering.
- Indian society endured and respected Shaka, Huna, Shithiyan, Muslim, Christian, Sikh, Jain, and Buddhist cultures.
- **According to Rigveda,** truth is one, even then the Scholars describe it in various forms. In the Gita, Lord Krishna says, those praying others are actually praying to me. This thought is the extreme of tolerance.
- The philosophy of **Sarva Dharma Sambhava,** **Vasudhaive Kutumbkam etc.** of ancient texts reflects the extent of tolerance and love.
- In India, all religions are respected equally, and Indian culture admits the manifoldness of reality and grasps the plurality of perspectives, customs, behaviours, and institutions. It does not attempt to suppress diversity in favour of uniformity. The motto of Indian culture is both unity in diversity as well as diversity in unity.
- Indians are generally known for their nonviolence, tolerance, brotherhood character; that is why several religions have flourished in Indian society.

## **Challenges to the Love and Tolerance**

- Various communal violence, conversion movements etc. shows intolerant attitudes of some people. For example: Violence after partition etc.
- The growing heinous crimes like murder, rape, corruptionetc.showslackoffeelingoflowtowards others.

## **Importance of Tolerance and Love in Present Time**

- With the growth of globalization, the whole world became a family. Thus, to maintain harmonious relations among countries’ values of tolerance and love is very important.
- Tocontaincrimes,violenceetc.amongpeoplethese values play a very important role in maintaining law and order and peace in the society.
- The growing inequality and poverty are also huge concerns, it requires love towards other people to reduce these and create a sense of responsibility among rich people for charity and public welfare.
- Climate change and global warming are the result of greedy behavior of people; this can be tackled effectively by enhancing these values in society. India is a land of love and tolerance, which holds its diverse people together. Without tolerance and harmony, the lasting peace of societies cannot be maintained, and loyalty for each other cannot be established. At a personal level the feelings of love strengthen the feelings of loyalty. When a citizen loves hiscountry,heexhibitsloyaltyanddevotionandmakes sacrifices for the sake of the nation. If sentiments of love do not exist, then the spirit of sacrifice will not be formed. Unless a person loves another, he can never have good feelings in his heart towards him, and he cannot faithfully fulfill the rights due to that person. Lack of tolerance leads to violence, and finally it destroys the peace and security of society.

## Question 129 (Year: 2017 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2017-gs1-q129-unacademy**
**Q19.Distinguish between religiousness/religiosity and communalism giving one example of how the former has transformed into the latter in independent India. (15 marks, 250 words) Tag: Society of India.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to start by defining** **religiosity and communalism.**-**In Body,**-**Write some difference between** **communalism and religiosity.**-**Discuss how religiosity has got** **transformed into communalism in** **independent India.**-**In Conclusion, summarize the relationship** **and suggest a way forward.** **Answer:** **Religiousness/Religiosity refers to** an individual’s conviction,devotion,andvenerationtowardsadivinity. Communalism is simply community-based politics and a newer phenomenon compared to religiosity. Religiosity is as old as religion and civilization itself.

## **Difference Between Communalism and Religiosity**

- Religiosity is concerned with religious aspects only, while communalism is a political ideology.
- **Communalism is more rigid than religiosity and** **viewed as a negative concept.** Communalism or communal ideology consists of three basic elements or stages. It is the belief that people who follow the same religion have political, economic, social and cultural interests. From this arises the notion of socio-political communities based on religion.
- The second stage of communal ideology rests on the notion that in multi-religious society like India the social, cultural, economic and political interests, of the followers of one religion are dissimilar and divergent from the interests of the followers of another
- The third stage of communalism is reached when theinterestsofthefollowersofdifferentreligionsor of different ‘communities’ are seen to be mutually incompatible, antagonistic and hostile. Thus, the communalist asserts this stage that Hindus and Muslims cannot have common secular interests, thattheirsecularinterestsareboundtobeopposed to each other **How Religiosity has got Transformed into**

## **Communalism in Independent India**

- Communalism emerged as a consequence of the emergence of modern politics which marked a sharp break with the politics of the ancient or medieval or pre-1857 periods. British rule and its policy of Divide and Rule bore special responsibility for the growth of communalism in modern India.
- After independence two most important factors fuelled communalism were Firstly, the partition deeply wounded the people psyche, Secondly, the resurgence of Hindu-Muslim economic competition fuelled a communal ideology.
- Political parties, prompted by political considerations, take decisions, which promote communal violence. Communal riots have also taken place after independence. For example: Ram Mandir & Babri masjid demolition issue, cow slaughter issue etc. Religiosity teaches humanism, tolerance on the other hand communalism has seeds To bifurcate a nation. So, for peaceful coexistence in a diversified nation like ours we should always promote the ideas of religiosity not the idea of communalism. The government programs like **Ek Bharat Shreshtha** **Bharatetc.** arerightstepsinthedirectionofcommunal harmony in society.
[Question ID: 2017-gs1-q129-unacademy]

## Question 130 (Year: 2017 | Paper: GS I | Marks: 10)

**Question ID: 2017-gs1-q130-unacademy**
**Q20.“ThegrowthofcitiesasI.T.hubshaveopenedup new avenues of employment but have also created new problems”. Substantiate this statement with examples. (15 marks, 250 words) Tag: Geography.**

#

## Answer

## **Decoding the Question**

- **In Introduction, try to write about the IT** **industry.**-**Discuss the role of the IT industry in** **generating employment.**-**Write some problems associated with IT** **industries.**-**In Conclusion, write its overall importance** **of IT industries.** **Answer:**The Information Technology (IT) industry is an essential component of the technology-driven knowledgeeconomyofthe21stcentury.Infact,globally India has been**recognised as a knowledge economy**due to its impressive growth. It is instrumental in**creating infrastructure** to store, process and exchange information for important business operations and other organisations. This industry has a conspicuous impact in improving the productivity of almost every other sector of the economy, it also has huge potential for further accelerating the growth and economic development.

## **Role of IT industry in Generating Employment**

- All the sub-sectors of this industry have made strides in revenue growth in the last two decades and fuelled the growth of the Indian economy.
- The rapid advancement within the IT industry and liberalisation policies such as reducing trade barriers and eliminating import duties on technology products by the Government of India are instrumental in the growth of this industry.
- IT sector created 8.73 lakh new jobs in the last five years as per the **Ministry of Electronics and IT** **(MeitY) report.** It is very crucial component for employment generation.
- Trickle-down effect from this industry has created many more job opportunities and development of the surroundings.
- **AccordingtotheNationalAssociationofSoftware** **and Service Companies (NASSCOM)** IT industry continues to be a net hirer and around 2.5-3 million new jobs will be created by 2025.

## **Problems Associated with IT Industries**

- These IT hubs in Bangalore, Hyderabad etc., have experienced population outburst. With an estimated population of 8.5 million in 2011, Bengaluru is India’s third most populous city. It was the fastest-growing Indian metropolis after Delhi between 1991 and 2001, with a growth rate of 38% during the decade. **Overpopulation will** **invite over-crowding, unemployment, pollution,** **etc.**- As the World Bank notes, the city’s economic success is creating**infrastructure problems,** **including poor water quality, traffic congestion** **and housing shortages.** Water is a particular problem with 30% of city residents using polluted groundwater. The sewer system does not reach a large part of the city. Average commuting times exceed40minutesbecausejobsaredispersedfrom the city centre.
- These are creating many health issues due to extended work timings, work stress, competition etc. These are also leading causes of lifestyle diseases like obesity, change in sleep cycle etc. With over half the world population now living in cities, mass transport and renewable energy are becoming even more important, as are the growth of new industries and information and communication technologies. Technological progress is also key
<p align="center"><img src="images/unacademy_p650_img34.png" alt="Diagram" /></p> to finding lasting solutions to both economic and environmental challenges, such as providing new jobs and promoting energy and efficiency. Promoting sustainable industries and investing in scientific research and innovation are all important ways to **facilitate sustainable development.**So, the only way forward here is to practice sustainable development goals (SDG 9) while growing IT hubs.**ANSWERS AND EXPLANATION** ---
